ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u16	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present my position on the print project No.3551, which contains amendments to three laws: the Act on income tax on individuals, the Family Benefits Act and the Act on the provision of health care financed by public funds. The exemption limit is linked to the failure to exceed the first tax threshold, i.e. the amount of PLN 85 528 per year. This year, as the Act will enter into force on 1 August - this is the plan - this amount is proportionally lower and amounts to PLN 35 636. Unfortunately, I must point out that the project that has been presented is burdened with a certain lie. Besides, this is not the first such project from the Law and Justice Government. I must say that very often, when discussing financial laws, my colleagues ask me when I report on the project, which is the second bottom. Well, in this case, I don't want to talk about the second day, but there's an understatement here. The Law and Justice government has declared income tax exemption - everyone has heard it - young people up to the age of 26, but as usual, the devil is in the details. This project excluded people up to 26 years of age who are self-employed, self-employed or in a small company, but their own company, i.e. the most active people, to whom we often put such a task, to take matters into their own hands. Unfortunately, this does not apply to these persons, because the Act states that income from business relationships, employment relationships, overlay work, cooperative employment relationships and contract contracts are exempt from income tax on individuals. My dear fellows, in this case the project violates the principle of equality before the law, it is not for everyone, although we are preaching something completely different. The government emphasizes that young people are released until they are 26 years old, but it does not prove that this is selective. In fact, these young people, who very often already have an idea during their studies and implement it - maybe small, because it is a combination of science and business - we exclude. This project cannot be rejected because it is a good option, but, as I say, it is burdened with a lie. And that's a lie - I hope - in the course of committee work we just have to eliminate it, we have to fix it, we have to extend it to those groups that haven't been included. These are the groups of the most active people we should particularly care about. The idea is that these people, starting their own business at a young age, should be able to continue it in the following years. So we will certainly make an amendment on this issue. The second point concerns the failure to take account of compensation for local government units. Calling this in such a way that it is the participation of local government units and going out to meet the young, is, I would say, a little naive, because it is not the first task that the local government must carry out on its own. The cost for the budget, as has been said, is: in the following years, the budget will not be affected by PLN 2.5 billion. So we are not going to reject this project, but we are paying attention, as I said, to these two issues that need to be taken into account: the question of extending this project and including all young people up to the age of 26 and the question of compensation for local government units. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u22	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position towards the government's draft law amending the Act on Income Tax on Individuals, the Law on Family Benefits and the Act on Health Care Services financed by public funds. This bill is part of the bill announced in February this year. so-called. Five of Law and Justice. After so many promises made, no one should be surprised that the present government is fulfilling another. This time we are discussing a solution that benefits taxpayers, which is zero PIT for young people. A new tax credit is to be granted to people up to the age of 26 who receive income based on the most popular forms of employment - so-called. job or contract order - regardless of the number of contracts signed. The exemption from personal income tax will be limited by an amount limit of PLN 85 528 in the tax year. The excess income will be taxed on a general basis, taking into account the tax-free amount and the preferential rules available to spouses and single parents. The amendments are to enter into force on 1 August 2019. It is important to emphasise that the proposed rules aim not only to increase the wages of workers fulfilling the criteria set out in the Act, but also to reduce the employment costs of those in this social group, which will increase their attractiveness in the labour market. Work without PIT is, of course, more in the pockets of young people, but it is also savings for employers, for whom such an employee is simply cheaper. It is also important that the beneficiaries of these solutions are often those who refuse to work with science on a daily basis, which makes it very difficult for them to achieve financial independence. These are people for whom the facilitation provided for in these regulations can provide an additional incentive to remain in our country, to start a career here or to continue it. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports this bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u78	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings in the administration and some other laws, print no. 3379. The need to amend the Law of 17 June 1966 on enforcement proceedings in the administration in the section on enforcement costs arises from the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal delivered on 28 June 2016. The Constitutional Tribunal found that the article was incompatible. 64 § 1 point 4 and § 6 Laws based on Art. 2 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland is a principle of the prohibition of excessive interference in connection with Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 84 Constitution of the Republic of Poland and Art. 64 § 8 the Act in so far as it does not provide for the possibility of reducing the fee referred to in Article. 64 § 1 point 4 of that law, and the handling fee in the event of termination of the proceedings due to the voluntary payment of the enforceable debt after enforcement of the action resulting from the article. 2 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland is a principle of the prohibition of excessive interference in connection with Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 84th Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Taking into account the guidelines of the Constitutional Tribunal, the draft amends the rules governing enforcement costs in a comprehensive manner by establishing new rules for the calculation and collection of them. The joint meeting of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights and the Committee on Public Finance on 12 June this year examined in detail the government draft of the bill, during which Parliamentary Club Law and Justice tabled 10 amendments. The first two amendments were clear. Amendment 3 introduces a general regulation on how documents drawn up in electronic form can be authenticated. Amendment 4 is to add a more detailed definition relating to the scope of the information contained in the request for re-introduction of administrative execution. Amendment 5 gives new wording § Article 2 64a Act in order to avoid a legislative glitch. Amendment 6 consists in replacing the words "from day to day' in Article. 1 in point 22. Amendment 7 consists in adapting the Article. 135 to the amendments resulting from the repeal of Article. 64d and addition of new provisions in Article. 64ca, Art. 64cb, Art. 64ce and Art. 64a regulating these enforcement costs. Amendment 8 consists in deleting the first sentence, which will allow enforcement and protection proceedings before the date of entry into force of the law not only of the first sentence of Article. 60 § 1, but also the second sentence, which is not subject to the amendments provided for in the project in question. Amendment 9 aims to harmonise charging arrangements. Amendment 10 consists in changing the words "after the date of entry into force' to "after the entry into force'. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this bill, together with the amendments adopted during the vote. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u79	Thank you. And I invite Mrs Katarzyna Osos, Civic Platform - Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u80	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Club, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings in the administration and some other laws. The draft law is a response to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, according to which the provisions of the Enforcement Act in the administration do not adequately determine the maximum amount of the enforcement fee and the handling fee, and to the extent that they do not provide for the possibility of reducing the enforcement fee and the handling fee for the remission of proceedings due to the voluntary payment of the enforcement fee after enforcement, are incompatible with the Constitution. At the same time, the Court pointed out that, for the proper implementation of the judgment, the legislator should determine the maximum amount not only of those fees but also of other enforcement fees, and how these fees should be calculated in the event of redemption of the proceedings in connection with the voluntary payment of the enforcement fee after enforcement. Thus, the proposed regulation provides for the setting of two amounts of the handling fee, regardless of the amount of the debt covered by the implementing title, collected separately from each implementing title, the introduction of a enforcement fee levied in proportion to the cash being enforced, the reduction in the amount of the enforcement fee in case of voluntary payment of the enforcement fee, the non-imposition of enforcement costs for a creditor operating within a national budgetary unit or a state budgetary unit, the extension of the enforcement expenditure catalogue, the amendment of the conditions for the remission of enforcement costs and the payment of such costs in instalments, the introduction of provisions governing the limitation of enforcement costs and the cancellation of the enforcement fee, and the modification of procedures for the reopening of administrative executions resulting from the amended provisions on enforcement costs. During the committee's work, it was pointed out that this project actually provides for a significant increase in costs in enforcement proceedings in the administration. This project raises all costs and hopes that the fees are not separated from the real expenditure incurred by the authorities. However, given that the draft is the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Court, we will support this proposal together with the amendments of the committee. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u90	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the government draft law on the system of development institutions, print No 3479. During the first reading of this draft law, the purpose of its adoption was set. I would like to remind you that this law is intended to regulate the rules and strengthen cooperation between the main institutions of the State implementing measures for economic and social development through the use of economic instruments. The aim will be to adopt mechanisms to ensure coordination and complementarity of the following entities: Polish Development Fund SA, Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, Polish Agency for Enterprise Development, Insurance Corporation of Export Credits SA, Polish Investment and Trade Agency SA and Industrial Development Agency SA. The proposed concept of establishing a system of development institutions operates in many European Union countries: France, the UK, Germany, Italy, Luxembourg, Bulgaria, Spain, Hungary, Latvia and Slovakia. At the joint meeting of the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development on 13 June this year. was discussed in detail the draft law from print No 3479. At the Parliamentary Club meeting, Law and Justice tabled seven amendments. The first amendment aims to add a provision which sets out in general the tasks of the Council of the Polish Development Fund. The second amendment aims to clarify the procedure for updating the strategy. The third amendment allows disclosure of information covered by professional secrecy within the meaning of this law at the request of the President of the CSO. Amendment 4 is more specific. In the event of an increase in costs for contractors performing a contract under a public contract, these entrepreneurs will be able to apply for a change in remuneration due to an increase in the cost of performance of the contract. Amendment 5 aims to clarify that the provisions of the Commercial Companies Code apply to mergers of companies in scope which are not regulated by the Act. The sixth amendment aims to clarify that the financial support provided by the National Capital Fund S.A. applies to existing provisions when a subsidiary is replaced by the National Capital Fund. Amendment 7 introduces the following Article. 39 (1) (a) 3: the authority competent to monitor the use of the expenditure limit referred to in paragraph 3. 1 and the implementation of the corrective mechanism referred to in paragraph 1. 2 is the Minister responsible for State budget matters. The Law and Justice Club will support this bill, along with the amendments, in the votes. Thank you very much. We count on this - I refer to the OSR annexed to the Act in this respect - that the solutions proposed in the Act will, indirectly, of course, because this project does not directly affect individual entrepreneurs only state institutions, but indirectly affects innovative companies by providing financing for start-up and development, increasing the potential for financing innovative venture capital ideas, so-called. venture capital, for a total amount of PLN 3/4 billion over the next 4 years of the Act, the development of an ecosystem to promote an innovation culture, among others. by providing access to expertise related to the ecosystem for the development of innovation, human capital development, access to skilled personnel and networks, both for entities investing in innovative ideas and innovative companies, and support for foreign expansion. I would still like to stress one thing which was said in the first question by the Member. We do not interfere, we do not cut or modify the budgets of individual institutions. So if someone uses it today, for example. with support or with PARP assistance, very often they are entrepreneurs who use PARP funds or e.g. with the support of PAIH exports, it should not feel this law directly. Indirectly yes, because we want to coordinate the activities of these institutions, but it is not that we modify, e.g., let's give for it, now the PARP will have less money, and PAIH more, these are not such shifts. From the perspective of an entrepreneur using the PARP or PAIH fund, there should be no such negative effects. Mr President asked what it looks like in other countries. Of course, we, if you can say so, are not the first to bring in something like this. whether they are trying to create such a system of development institutions. It seems to me that the most important examples are, of course, Germany and France. Most importantly, because these are very large European economies and capitalism there, so to speak, it has grown deeper into roots. In Germany it is the German Kreditanstalt für Wiederaufbau, which, as far as I know, is currently the largest development institution in the world. It was created after World War II as part of Marshall's plan: 80% of state control, 20% national German trade unions. It employs 6,000. persons. As far as its assets are concerned, there is nothing to be deceived about, they are much larger assets than those held by the Polish institutions, because this is 500 billion euros, and there is also a statutory basis, by the way. As far as the French system is concerned, interestingly, he is even older, because it dates back to just after Napoleonic times, the French State appointed him in the beginning to support the saving and construction of French capital, that was in 1816. There is a deposit bank and then an investment bank is attached to this system. Currently, the consolidated value of the assets of the bank is EUR 170 billion. The bank has 130,000. employees, which makes it one of the oldest institutions and one of the largest development institutions in the world. So these are some of the patterns that we've looked at or referred to by shaping this new law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u115	Thank you. Mr Włodzimierz Nykiel, Civic Platform - Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u116	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club, I have the honour to present an opinion on the draft law amending the law on the monitoring system for road and rail freight transport and certain other laws, print No 3474 and 3534. According to the declaration of the designer, the purpose of the proposed law is to seal the tax system for the trading of fuel oil and diesel fuel intended for fuel purposes and other liquid fuel fuels which, due to their use for fuel purposes, are subject to a reduced excise duty. The stated aim of the law is also to reduce administrative burdens for entrepreneurs, although it must be said that this is a side objective. It seems that such a legislative initiative should involve a dissemination of knowledge in society about the proposed changes. The design of the proposed rules may lead to the conclusion that the sales of fuel oils will be burdened with the burden of informing about the new rules, as they will be affected by the greatest consequences of possible non-compliance, including by other operators. The first effect of the new legislation is the need for the oil user to make a declaration containing, inter alia, an indication of the place where the heating equipment is located and the estimated quantity of fuel oils consumed in the calendar year. The absence of any minimum volume limit for the monitoring of the turnover of fuel oils should be assessed negatively, except for the quite laconic statement in the justification of the project that the malfunctions in the trading of fuel oils justify the extension of the operation of the SENT system. It has not been clarified how even small deliveries will prevent large-scale irregularities from being covered by monitoring. In view of the principle of proportionality, which requires State authorities to use only such measures as are necessary to achieve a specific objective, and the fact that public authorities should achieve the objective at the least cost for that State and individual individuals, it should be considered that the rules introduced are disproportionate to the expected effects. It is difficult to conclude that irregularities in the acknowledgement of receipt by the recipient of several litres of fuel oil as regards the absence of closure of the SENT notification justify the imposition of a fine of 5 000 on the seller. zł. It's just a disproportionately high punishment. The penalties imposed should be proportionate to the infringements found and the project does not take this into account. The lack of further reflection by the authorities on the imposition of penalties on existing rules is already evidenced by judicial and administrative case law. The practice of the authorities indicates that current penalties for irregularities found are imposed as if automatically regulated by the Act. The authorities do not care that this may result in the end of business activity by the trader. We positively assess the objectives of the law, agree with the need for action in this area, but do not accept the shortcomings of the regulation that I have allowed you to present. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u130	Dear Marshal, Dear Members, High Izbo! Thank you very much for these questions relating to the very important law, in our opinion, because concerning the next stage of sealing in the field of liquid fuels. Answering specific questions - the first question is Mr Piotr Pyzik's question as to whether the imposition of obligations will involve the need for computer equipment or Internet connection. I reply immediately that we have tried as little as possible to burden both entrepreneurs and natural persons who may be recipients of fuel oils by applying these regulations. Therefore, the solutions adopted mean that none of the recipients will have to have computer equipment or internet connection. The delivery confirmation will follow on the supplier's equipment using a unique PIN code that will hold each customer. I would therefore stress that this does not necessarily involve any costs incurred by the customers of fuel oil, which will be subject to monitoring. As regards the question about vacathio legis, the transitional period when recipients and suppliers will be able to use the old system of paper declarations, I would like to draw attention to the issue which has been resounding here all the time during the presentation of the club positions by the honourable ladies and gentlemen, the issue of sealing the system. I stress once again that this is a law that is aimed primarily at the digital society. We depart from paper documents, which were actually a heavy burden, also a financial burden, for those who had to fulfill it and later for 5 years to store it, but nevertheless we want to do it in an evolutionary way, so we have adopted a very long transitional period, because, I stress, until 31 March 2020. At this time, of course, information campaigns, information campaigns are planned, and I will say that they are already underway. This law has been very intensively consulted with associations that bring together suppliers of fuel oil. In Poland we have two large associations representing the general liquid fuel industry. These associations have already started an information campaign for their members, so that there is absolutely no fear that this information will not reach these people for such a long time. I stress once again that these responsibilities are actually incumbent on suppliers, not on recipients, and therefore there is no risk that this pool of people to whom this information has to be reached is very large. In fact, this applies to a relatively small segment of entities specialising in this industry, which have called for the introduction of these rules precisely because of fair competition, because they want to rid the market of unfair entities. As for the question of whether such a system exists somewhere. It can be said that Poland is the forerunner here. Indeed, there is a system similar to that of properties in Hungary, but I stress that it is a system based on outdated technology of bouncers, i.e. a little equivalent of our viaTOLL system. Our system is based primarily on a unique technology that everyone downloads for free, uploads into a smartphone and can use it. It can therefore be said that we are a precursor both in Europe and in the world. Let me be clear that this system raises great interest in other countries. Many times I have participated in various meetings such as the Visegrad Group and I know that our colleagues from neighbouring countries are very interested in this system and are also considering introducing it. I can also tell you today: just today was the presentation of a report on the grey zone by one of the United Nations agencies. This agenda clearly praises this system, I will say more, and praises the amendment that the High House is discussing today. So we can see that even the United Nations appreciates the potential that this bill has. Finally, the last question, by Mr Mirosław Suchon, concerning the issue of punishment. I have already said the same in a high committee meeting, and I would like to stress this once again: this punishment, which is potentially imposed, does not concern fuel oil. That 10,000. zloty is a penalty, a potential penalty, in the case of the introduction of provisions relating to LPG - a regulation is being examined - and it concerns the writing of meters; the aim is not to avoid monitoring through the SENT system. Once again, this punishment, 10,000. zł, not due to lack of analysis, only this results from the value of the potential autocister of the standard LPG station in Poland, which has two tanks. The reduction in public debt is just around 10,000. zł. This is a fundamental principle, which is always accepted in every country to fight economic crime - economic criminals cannot pay for illegal activities. If this punishment is less than 10,000. zł, it is very simple, criminals will continue to be profitable because they will pay it as part of their operations, you can say, and they will still make a profit. That's why the government is standing up to keep that amount. I stress once again: this will not make it worthwhile not to make a supplementary notification in the form of writing LPG counters - but this does not apply to entrepreneurs who operate on the fuel oil market. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u134	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report to the committee on the Government's draft law amending the Law on the greenhouse gas emissions trading scheme and certain other laws, print No 3471 and 3569. High Izbo! This draft law implements four Directives of the European Parliament and of the Council: Directive 2010/75/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 24 November 2010 on industrial emissions, Directive 2015/2193 of 25 November 2015 on the limitation of emissions of certain pollutants from medium combustion plants, Directive 2016/2284 of 14 December 2016 on the reduction of national emissions of certain atmospheric pollutants and Directive 2018/410 of 14 March 2018, which amends the 2003 Directive to strengthen cost-effective emission reductions and low-carbon investments. This draft law also implements two regulations of the European Parliament and of the Council: 2017/2392 and 2018/2067. High Izbo! The issue of standardising emissions trading and ensuring that the system is coherent and efficient requires continuous improvement. I would like to remind you that three years have passed since these rules were adopted in 2015, so we already have some comparative material and experience in applying these rules in practice. This draft law is precisely an adjustment after 3 years of practical application of the rules. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 63 amendments to the Law on the greenhouse gas emissions trading scheme are introduced. Changes include: in addition to the definition and clarification of the rules, so that their interpretation does not give rise to any doubt that they are properly clarified. These changes extend the deadlines, which takes into account the real time needed to carry out the action in question for the good functioning of the emissions trading scheme. This is intended to allow the balancing of greenhouse gas emission allowances issued for other investment tasks in the event that the investor ceases to carry out any task or has not achieved approved conformity indicators. There are also new rules for fixing emission allowances during the trading period starting from 2021. And, of course, the point is to find new sources of stable funding for the tasks of the National Centre for Balancing and Emissions Management by allocating PLN 8 million annually from revenues generated from the sale of emission allowances through auctions, and this requires the transfer of these funds from the state budget to the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and the increase of the maximum limit of the expenditure of the fund for financing the KoBIZ. More or less, the amendments contained in the article are based on this. 1 Act on the greenhouse gas emissions trading scheme. Art. 2 contains amendments to the Law of 17 June 1966 on enforcement proceedings in the administration. There is a supplement to the grounds for administrative enforcement in respect of environmental charges. Art. 3 in turn contains amendments to the Environmental Protection Inspection Act of 20 July 1991. These changes, which are included there, carry out an extension of the current air quality monitoring by two stations in the Małopolska and Zachodniopomorskie voivodships. The law also adds provisions that define the responsibility of the authorities for the transmission of monitoring reports to the European Commission and the European Environment Agency. High Izbo! In turn, the article. 5 contains amendments to the Act of 29 August 1997. Bank law. This is a minor change, but it is important because there is a deprivation of the national administrator's entitlement to require the bank to provide information that is a bank secret about the operator. Change, as I say, is short, but important. The next amendment is Art. 12, these are quite extensive changes to the law on greenhouse gas emission management and other substances. Firstly, certain information and reporting obligations are extended on the part of the National Centre for Balancing and Emissions Management. There is an obligation to develop a national air pollution reduction programme. Two new categories of substances are also added - these are HFCs and other PFCs - and the Annex to the Act is amended accordingly. There is also an extension of certain information and reporting obligations by operators. Another change is to clarify the tasks of national inventory and emission projections and introduce tasks that involve the preparation of methodological reports. The task of providing KOBIZE is to draw up periodic forecasts of changes in activity for individual sectors of the economy. These are more or less, of course, the points raised in the article. 12 amending the Law on the greenhouse gas emission management system and other substances. I would also like to draw the attention of the High House to the changes made in the article. 9 of this project, in the Act of 10 July 2007 on Fertilisation and Fertilisation. The use of ammonium carbonate mineral fertilisers is prohibited. This is the implementation of the tasks resulting from the relevant directive - the NEC Directive and I think this is a kind of analogy. Similarly, two years ago in Water Law, we introduced provisions of the Nitrates Directive, i.e. such provisions in national legislation, which are intended to reduce pollution which is introduced into the environment as a result of the use of fertilisers in agriculture. Technical solutions are also similar. Water law provides the basis for developing best practices, and a similar solution is also used in this law, i.e. provisions on the obligation to draw up a national advisory code of good agricultural practice to reduce ammonia emissions. As I say, it is related to this directive. High Izbo! There are also amendments to the Act of 3 October 2008 on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection, etc. These changes consist in adding to the list of documents made available by the Minister responsible for Environmental Affairs in the National Public Information Bulletin of the Programme for the Reduction of Air Pollution of KPOZP, including emission inventories, emission forecasts, methodological reports and reports concerning the location of monitoring stations and data obtained from these stations. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 in point 27 in Article. 40a paragraph. 8 and 9 are making a new sound. I will not read it, the amendment will be made officially after the end of my speech. It's for the mouth. 9 we add the mouth. 10. Justification in a few sentences: The proposed change makes the derogation mechanism for energy more flexible. In the current state, an installation may receive free allowances for balancing the value of investment expenditure incurred on investments originally included in the national investment plan, the NIP. This is a list of investments that energy companies can make, approved by the European Commission, and must not be subject to any change. Where the investment has been started and the expenditure on it has been used to balance free emission allowances but has ceased to be implemented, the company should ask the State budget for the financial equivalent of the emission allowances received linked to the costs of this particular investment. The aim of the amendment is to create the possibility of balancing previously received emission allowances by imposing on other investment tasks included in the NIP without having to ask for their equivalence. Some companies have entered into the KPI more investments, which gives them an excess on investment contributions. The proposed amendment aims to require the Minister responsible for the environment to provide copies of documents relating to proceedings not only to the National Centre for Balancing and Emissions Management but also to the European Commission. Second amendment. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 52 after the words "from the date of publication, replace the comma with a dot and delete the words "with the exception of Article." Point 1 (27). The essence of this amendment is that, according to the earlier proposal, we had to wait for the European Commission's decision to start anything. However, after these amendments, which will probably be put to the vote together, we will inform the Commission without delay, but we are not waiting for a decision, but we are implementing it in practice - unless it objects, then we will come back later, but there is no way that the case is frozen and we are waiting for the decision of the European Commission. This is the important change. On behalf of the club, we support the bill with these amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

All these activities are to be carried out in accordance with the principle of sustainable development, with a view to establishing the cultural heritage of this place appropriate functions in collective life and social awareness. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u266	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies, print No 3545 on the Government's draft law amending the law on the promotion of the development of telecommunications services and networks and some other laws, seym print No 3484. The project entered the Sejm on 28 May 2019 and Mr Marshal addressed it to the first reading in the Committee on Digitization, Innovation and Modern Technologies on 3 June. On 10 June, the committee received a positive opinion on the draft law from the Office for Sejm Analysis. The first reading was held on 26 June. Drafting amendments were proposed during the first reading, which were notified by the Legislative Bureau. The amendments were accepted by the committee. I would like to say a few words about this extremely important project. The aim of the draft law on amending the law on the promotion of the development of telecommunications services and networks and some other laws is to increase the access of citizens and entrepreneurs to modern telecommunications services. In particular, the further expansion of modern fibre networks, especially in digitally excluded areas, i.e. rural and suburban areas. In a broader context, the aim is to enable our country and economy to develop through digitalisation and modern technologies. The project concerns inter alia, firstly, the acceleration of the investment and construction process for telecommunications infrastructure, secondly, the reduction of construction costs and the operation of modern telecommunications networks, thirdly, the increase of the possibility of using existing technical infrastructure for telecommunications purposes, including the reduction of access costs. I have to admit that a government-designed project solves many problems and eliminates those barriers that have been identified for more than 10 years and have not been solved so far, such as. the reduction of maximum tolls for the occupation of road lane for telecommunications infrastructure. The Ministry of Digitalization was able - it is worth noting - to get a positive opinion from the local government on these actions, these changes. A draft law besides the liquidation of the so-called. White spots on fibre access also apply to mobile networks. In this respect, it responds in particular to the conclusions of the Supreme Audit Chamber taken at its meeting on the basis of a recent audit of public authorities' activities in the field of protection against electromagnetic radiation from mobile telephone devices. The draft law takes into account the issues raised by NIK, which only demonstrates the completeness and need of these regulations. At the first reading of the draft law in the committee, the government presented the justification of the project fairly and it should be fully agreed with Minister Wanda Buk that it is a modern law for modern time. Therefore, on behalf of the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technology, I recommend that the High Sejm adopt the report in accordance with the submission. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u268	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I would like to present my position on the draft law amending the law on the promotion of the development of telecommunications services and networks and certain other laws. The aim of the project is to increase citizens' access to modern telecommunications services and broadband Internet access eliminates digitally excluded areas, including rural areas. Digitisation provides the economy with growth and stimulates growth in areas without these opportunities. The solutions adopted are based on the objectives of the Responsible Development Strategy by 2020 (with a view to 2030) and the recommendation of the European Commission Communication Links to a competitive digital single market: towards a European gigabit society. Access to broadband internet should be made widely available by 2015 at a much higher rate than so far. The project contains solutions that will facilitate and speed up the implementation of the investment and construction process. It contains regulations that provide access to information on electromagnetic radiation emissions in the environment in connection with the construction and modernisation of mobile networks. The draft law is compatible with European Union law. The proposed regulation will not require notification to the European Commission under the Law of 30 April 2004 on State aid procedures. The proposal does not require it to be submitted to the competent authorities and institutions of the European Union, including the European Central Bank, in order to obtain an opinion, to notify, consult or agree. The Act published in the Official Journal will be passed to the European Commission as a national implementing measure. On behalf of the club Law and Justice, I strongly recommend the above. the law and I submit nine amendments. Thank you. [[Applause]]

It is a network that will not only involve the possibility of using the faster Internet, but will also provide an opportunity to improve the quality of life of citizens. The fifth generation Internet will offer opportunities for developing telemedicine, public transport, use of autonomous cars or entertainment. Playing videos in 4k quality will be something that will be obvious thanks to this fast network. Therefore, it must be said explicitly that the fifth generation Internet is an opportunity, a great opportunity for development, also economic development, and not any threat. And that's what we have to say to each other directly. We need to take advantage of this opportunity that modern technologies give us. And in order to implement these technologies, we need to carry out well projects related to the construction of the network. The draft of this law has many assumptions that are worth paying attention to. Of course, those that are positive include, first, the reduction of the toll for the occupation of road traffic in the implementation of telecommunications investments, second, free access to the vertical infrastructure belonging to local authorities, such as lanterns, stops or road signs. Other issues are important. But attention should also be paid to aspects that require some clarification. He is appointed here, among others. so-called. The Wideband Fund and the Wideband Fund as an idea are right, but the law does not clearly specify which entities, to whom the money will be collected, will be transferred as part of the fund. This should be clarified and we will call for such an amendment. That's one thing. Secondly, as was pointed out in the public consultation process - telecoms companies have said about it - too much red tape is being proposed in the context of the reports on the inventory of these networks. So far this has been done once a year, so today, according to the new proposal, it will be done at least twice a year, which, of course, involves the destiny of special resources, not only human but also financial, to implement these issues, so it would be burdensome for entrepreneurs. And since the law talks about supporting the development of services and networks, then why implement solutions that are not conducive to development, but even when introducing bureaucracy, they contribute to the fact that much more time and money must be spent on things that have nothing to do with the development of the network. And the third thing I'd like to draw your attention to. - the fifth generation of Internet cannot be implemented without changes in the impact of the magnetic field and without the definition of acceptable standards. Today, these standards are almost a hundred times higher than in many countries of the European Union and apply to the 1980s, when radio communications were somehow regulated by the communist regime and hence these restrictions were taken. This needs to be clearly changed. Until now, an integral part of the many draft laws that have entered the Sejm have been regulations. In this case, it's not there. This is interesting, because in order to take advantage of this opportunity and implement modern solutions, there must be clarity and certainty that these standards will be changed. This raises the question of how it will be, how it will be implemented and implemented in the future. With regard to details, of course, in the package of several amendments on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - the Civic Coalition I will pass on these comments, which concern, among others. issues raised by telecommunications undertakings and telecommunications chambers in the context of a public consultation. These are technical amendments that we will probably be discussing at the committee meeting. In conclusion, I would like to say that the Act offers great opportunities and better opportunities for the operation and construction of telecommunications networks, so we are inclined to support its project. At the same time, I'm handing over to your marshal a package of amendments, [[♪ The bell ♪]] Which I would like to justify during the committee's work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Mr Gonciusz concluded that people were afraid of the 5G network and asked if we had any knowledge of what the fifth generation of networks were like in other countries and if there was any chance that standards would be harmonised at European Union level. To answer your question... With regard to your conclusions, I would like to draw your attention to one thing. In fact, today this fear of the fifth generation network is generated, and it is so unjustified that if we are afraid of the fifth generation of networks, we should also be afraid of the fourth generation of networks, third generation networks, 2G networks and even 1G networks, because these are exactly the same physical phenomena that are used in all generations of networks. We can't underestimate our fears. Everyone has the right to be concerned, especially when it comes to his life and health. We have to answer them. But it's always worth to straighten it out. If we are afraid of the 5G network, then let us be afraid of mobile telephony at all, the telephone we have been using in Poland since the beginning of the 1990s, because nothing changes in this respect. Please note that in exactly the same way radio waves will be used after the introduction of the fifth generation network, which in large simplification is a network that simply has a different architecture, which does not focus on the construction of large base stations, but will function on the basis of micro- and picnic cells. What is the fifth generation of networks in other countries? More than 1 million users in South Korea today have the opportunity to use 5G networks. In several cities of the United States there is also this possibility. Today I read the information that in the coming year in Germany 20 cities are to be covered by the fifth generation network. Today, the fifth generation network is being launched in Switzerland. On the other hand, when it comes to our country, we are at the stage of pilot tests. Currently in Poland there are several tests, for example 17. The question was whether there would be harmonised standards at European Union level. I'm not familiar with these initiatives. This was left to the Member States for decision. I don't know anything about that when it comes to changing things. Mr Marchewka asked why there was no regulation on the intensity of the electromagnetic field. I've already answered that question. Is it necessary, in the case of 5G, to reduce the liberalisation of restrictive standards in force since 1984? Mr President, let me rephrase this. If we want to use the mobile telephone network, given how much data transmission increases, adapting the standards to the current state of research is necessary, even when using the 4G network. Today, most of you, entering our capital, probably experienced a situation when data transmission slows down. That's because the systems that work today are clogging themselves. They have a certain capacity, too. It was said here, I do not remember which of you said this, that 7 times from the very beginning the amount of data transmitted has increased. Mr President, I would like to say that this amount of data transmitted has increased 24 times. She will still grow really, really dynamically. [[30 times in 10 years.]] I answer Mr Man's question. Mr President, we have been discussing for a very long time the solution to the rates for already functioning networks. And there's one thing we have to say to each other here. If we introduced an obligation to lower rates for already built networks, but only for regional broadband networks, it would be public aid which needs notification. I do not rule out that such notification should be made. However, I admit that I do not fully agree with the concept of reducing all other operators who operate commercially, who at high rates have decided to invest in a given area, because this means that it was profitable for them. Of course, a completely different situation concerns regional broadband networks, which can only provide wholesale services which do not operate in this competitive market and which in their feasibility studies created at the turn of 2009 and 2010, as I recall, had set rates of 20 PLN. This is actually a huge handicap for them. We are aware that this must be dealt with. However, this will require notification. A number of questions were repeated in a large part. If I miss one, I promise we'll answer it in writing. Mr Ruszczyk asked about this one city, which according to the European Digital Agenda in 2020 should be covered by the fifth generation network. Ladies and gentlemen, we are planning activities related to the distribution of frequencies, mainly in the C-band, in the 3.6-3,8 GHz band, which is due to start at the end of this year and end at the latest in the middle of next year. In line with our current plans, we want to oblige operators in selection procedures to cover at least one city's fifth generation network. As a result, there may be more of these cities. In answer to Mr Ruszczyk's question, more simply stating that the fifth generation networks cannot be launched without the 700 band, I would like to point out that this is not true. As far as the 5G network is concerned, there's also a C band here, as I said, and there's a 26 GHz band. Fewly this, we can also use other frequency blocks in accordance with the principle of technological neutrality, provided that we do not adversely affect other functioning systems. This was more or less the case with the LTE network, which was launched on frequencies designed for 2G networks. This bill, just so we're clear, because it's been raining here many times, isn't a law that introduces 5G. I would very much like to write a bill in which I will write that we are introducing any new technology in Poland, give a 2-week vacathio legis and be glad that after 2 weeks it materializes. Ladies and gentlemen, this is not the case. If we are looking for any changes in the law system that would affect the implementation of the fifth generation of networks, it is clear that these were primarily changes in the telecoms law that we carried out at the beginning of this year with the approval of most of the countries that allowed for the cleaning of certain frequency bands. On the other hand, the amendment we are discussing today is an amendment that simply facilitates the development of the telecommunications network in general, so I will say the old Polish language. Ladies and gentlemen! With regard to Mr Lassota's question, which I had not told you before, which pointed out that the World Health Organisation classified the electromagnetic field as potentially carcinogenic, I would like to point out that the World Health Organisation provides for five categories of potentially carcinogenic factors. 2B, where the electromagnetic field is located, is the lightest of them. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u306	High Seym! Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the club PSL-UED, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the law on the promotion of the development of telecommunications services and networks and certain other laws. The main objective of the project is to increase access for citizens and entrepreneurs to modern telecommunications services, including broadband Internet, especially in digitally excluded areas, most often in rural areas. In a broader context, the aim is to provide incentives for the economy as a whole through effective digitalisation. The project provides for a package of solutions that will facilitate and speed up the implementation of an investment and construction process that is important for the development of modern telecommunications networks. It also includes regulations that provide access to information on electromagnetic radiation emissions in the environment in connection with the construction and modernisation of mobile networks. This is a response to the findings of the Supreme Audit Chamber resulting from the control of public administrations concerning protection against electromagnetic radiation from mobile devices. Among other things, it is envisaged. Accelerate the investment and construction process for telecommunications infrastructure (e.g. by clarifying the concepts of the Construction Law and by clarifying the procedure for reporting electromagnetic radiation installations and public participation in the process), as well as reducing the cost of construction and operation of modern telecommunications networks (e.g. by lowering the maximum toll rates for the occupation of the road lane and by reducing the fixed fee for the entry of service in the perpetual register). The PSL-UED Club is in favour of a bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u346	High Seym! Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the club PSL-UED, I will present my position on the government draft law amending the Act on Veterans of Activities outside the State and some other laws. The draft amendment envisages increasing aid for veterans and veterans affected. It is proposed to increase the amount of the allowance for the injured veteran and to grant the allowance to a group of injured veterans with a loss of health of l% up to 9%. The amount of the allowance shall be subject to a fixed percentage of the damage to health suffered as a result of an accident in connection with activities outside the borders of the State or a disease acquired during the performance of the tasks or duties of activities outside the borders of the State for which the compensation has been granted. The right to treatment has been offered outside the order and without a time limit to veterans affected by at least 30% of the damage to health for all diseases, even if they are not directly related to injuries or diseases arising from activities outside the country. Veterans whose health damage is at least 30% have been offered the right to participate once a year in free readaptational-conditional turns.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u355	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on information about the activities of the National Media Council and the National Radio and Television Council for 2018. In 2018, the National Media Council held 14 meetings. In order to ensure the functioning of the Polish Press Agency SA, the Council decided to elect Wojciech Surmacz as President of the Management Board of the Polish Press Agency SA. During this year the Council also dealt with the situation of Polish Radio SA and decided to change its statute. In connection with the suspension by the Supervisory Board of the President of the Board of Polish Radio and the delegation to perform the duties of the President of the Management Board of Andrzej Rogoyski, the Board also dealt with this situation. As a result of the actions taken, the National Media Council adopted a resolution on the dismissal of the President. Importantly, it was also decided to determine the minimum share of broadcasts created by the field branches of TV Polska Spółka Akcyjna in national programmes in 2019. In Program 1 it will be 254 hours, in Program 2 and 85 hours, and in TVP Polonia it will be 349 hours. In 2018, the National Media Council continued the process of setting up new programme councils for public broadcasting companies in 2017. By also exercising other competences arising from the provisions of the Act, the Council, among others, agreed to change the statutes, initiated a change in the statutes, agreed to appoint directors of the regional divisions of TVP. On several occasions, the Council held positions on matters important for the functioning of public media. In order to obtain detailed information on the matters of broadcasters, the National Media Council met with representatives of public companies of radio and television broadcasting, and especially often, which should be emphasized, representatives of the Polish Television SA. By safeguarding the wider public interest, members of the Council also met with representatives of different backgrounds. It is worth noting that at the time when the National Media Council was set up, for the first time in the history of law, two seats in a five-person opposition were guaranteed, which ensures pluralism and guarantees access to information about the activities of companies. In 2018, the National Council of Radiophony and Television implemented its competences during meetings, during which it adopted 347 resolutions in the field of its activities, submitted to the publication in the Official Journal of the 11 Regulations. As part of her participation in the legislative process in the field of radiophony and television, she gave an opinion on five draft laws and nine international agreements. Radio broadcasters received 13 new concessions to disseminate local programmes. The Chairman of the National Radio and Television Council issued 54 decisions on the so-called "Radio-TV'. the renewal of radio and television programmes. It also issued 24 licences for the dissemination of programmes in telecommunications networks, other than those used for the distribution of land-based or satellite-based spreaders, i.e. so-called. cable networks. 221 notifications were received by the National Radio and Television Council for inclusion in the register of television programmes only distributed in the ICT system and distributed programmes, which together with the notifications submitted at the end of 2017 were the basis for issuing 240 provisions on entry. The State budget for the activities of the National Radio and Television Council was allocated revenue of over PLN 38 million, which consisted inter alia. proceeds from concession fees. The website of the National Radio and Television Council visited a lot, because more than 340 thousand. users, generating almost 1,300,000 views. During the reporting period, within the reporting powers, the Council worked on issuing six regulations implementing laws, four of which were published in the Official Journal of the Laws. The work on the other two continued also in 2018. In 2018, the National Radio and Television Council also dealt with issues related to: procedures for agreeing with broadcasters of financial and programme plans projects undertaken to carry out missionary tasks; control of the use of subscription appropriations for public broadcasters for the financing of missionary programming activities; distribution between public companies of radio and television of PLN 980 million, which compensates for the proceeds of subscription fees lost in 2010 and 2017 as a result of statutory redundancies of certain social groups; monitoring of linear programmes and on-demand services; investigation of complaints and requests. Nearly 35 penalties have been imposed for abuse related, among others. exceeding the permitted time of advertising or concession violations. The National Radio and Television Council has also implemented a number of educational projects and social campaigns. It also operates at European level, working in institutions such as the Council of Europe, the European Regulators Group. audiovisual media services, Management Committee for media and information society or the European Audiovisual Observatory. We proceed with the examination of item 11 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and some other laws (print No 3541). I ask the Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Justice of Minister Łukasz Piebiak to present the justification for the bill. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u437	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed bill amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and certain other laws contained in Sejm Print No 3541, aims to create the basis for dematerialisation of shares of public limited liability companies and limited liability companies which are not subject to compulsory dematerialisation within the meaning of the Public Offering Act and the Act on Trading in Financial Instruments and therefore of companies not public limited liability companies. Dematerialisation of shares will contribute to increasing the safety and efficiency of trading in shares and simplifying the legal structures associated with this trading by enabling easy identification of the person entitled to the share. The trading of dematerialised shares will also increase transparency and the efficiency of the exchange of information in tax matters in accordance with OECD standards. The tax administration will be able to obtain and exchange information on shareholders of public limited liability companies and limited liability companies holding bearer shares. The current construction of bearer shares makes Poland a high-risk country which does not guarantee the exchange of tax information on bearer shares because it is not possible to identify them. This can lead, among others. to abuse the trading of shares, e.g. as part of money laundering. The project provides for the collection of data on shareholders and shares held by them in the shareholder register kept on the basis of a contract concluded separately by each joint-stock company and limited-liability company with an entity authorised to maintain securities accounts. This solution guarantees the reliability of keeping the register and the reliability of the data disclosed in it. Alternatively, each company may choose to register shares in accordance with the rules of the current public companies, i.e. recording of shares in the securities deposit. Both bearer shares and registered shares will be dematerialised. With regard to bearer shares, it must be stated that their construction does not serve to hide the shareholder but to facilitate the performance of the function of the bearer shares as a bearer security. As a result of the dematerialisation of the shareholders of the public limited liability company with a small number of shareholders, and such companies in Poland are the most, they will obtain from the point of view of identification a status similar to those of the limited liability company. There is no justification to guarantee the anonymity of shareholders of small public limited liability companies where such guarantees do not exist for limited liability companies. In respect of registered shares, dematerialisation means the elimination of the risks associated with disputes around entries in the public record in particular in conflict situations between shareholders and in operations of hostile acquisitions of companies. The dematerialisation of registered shares will constitute another natural step in the digitisation of the stock book, which is based on the current article. 341 § 8 of the Commercial Companies Code could be carried out in the form of electronic recording and on the basis of Article. 342 of this law could be carried out by a bank or an investment firm. The non-dematerialisation of registered shares would result in companies having to maintain two separate systems for registration of rights from shares - the register of shareholders for bearer shares and the book of shares for registered shares - which would not only cause nuisance for companies, but would also increase the costs of their operation and complicate the legal environment. Dematerialisation will introduce a single regime for the transfer of rights from shares, regardless of whether registered shares or bearer shares are traded, which will create the possibility of reducing costs and increasing the safety of trading in shares of non-listed companies. This regime will be based primarily on the disclosure function - in the case of non-public companies - in the shareholder register. The entry in the shareholder register is an analogy to the issue of the document and is therefore important not only for the identification of the person entitled to the shares but also for the transfer of rights from shares outside the stock exchange. As a result of the dematerialisation, the status of shares of non-public companies will be brought closer to the status of shares of public companies which are the subject of a public offer to be admitted to trading on a regulated market or introduced into an alternative trading venue. Dematerialisation of shares of non-public companies and full dematerialisation of shares of public companies is a sign of time. It should be noted that the existing rules provide for optional dematerialisation of securities such as bonds, investment certificates or bank securities. The absence of provisions allowing the dematerialisation of shares should therefore be considered as anachronism. This draft law is opposed to the development of information technology. The benefits of dematerialisation of shares resulting from the elimination of costs arising from the need to print, store and transport document securities should be highlighted. The introduction of a dematerialised form of shares reduces the risks associated with the loss of securities, their getting into the wrong hands or unauthorized activities involving documentary securities. This risk applies in particular to bearer securities for which the confidence in trading expressed in the ability to acquire rights from those securities is broadly protected, in good faith, in accordance with the rules laid down in the Civil Code. Dematerialisation reduces the risks associated with the disposal of securities and thus reduces the scope of cases where it is necessary to consider acquisition from unauthorised ones. This is mainly due to the waiver of the document form of the security, but also to the entrusting of keeping registers or accounts to qualified entities subject to State supervision. Those entities shall keep records and accounts in such a way as to minimise the risk of incorrect records and defective transfers. The dematerialisation of shares and the existence of a register kept by qualified entities, which are supervised by the Financial Supervision Commission, allow the use of the existing continuously improved and tested IT infrastructure for more than 20 years, as well as the procedures applied to public company shares, and consequently reduce to a minimum the risks associated with the acquisition of shares from an unauthorised person. The project also provides for the obligation for public limited liability companies and limited liability companies to operate their own website. The purpose of such an obligation is to facilitate communication between companies and their shareholders, since the obligation to maintain a website is linked to the obligation to publish notices from the company on this website. It is also required to disclose the address of the party in the National Court Register. The introduction of a solution providing for the conduct by all public limited liability companies, not only public ones, the website will increase the effectiveness of the protection of minority shareholders' rights, reduce the risk of information in such a way as not to inform shareholders in practice, which is particularly common in situations of conflicts in shareholders' and management companies, and promote stability and safety of trading, which is a systemic value. Mrs. Marshal! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, The proposed legal solutions implement OECD recommendations for the existing trading of bearer shares in Poland by allowing reliable information about the holder of the shares by both the company, the trading participants and the tax administration, which will improve the effectiveness of the system against money laundering and terrorist financing, and eliminating the risk of classification of Poland internationally to countries not meeting the standards of exchange of information, together with the so-called group. tax havens. Therefore, I ask the High House to support this proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u438	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Marek Ast will be the first to speak on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. Come on in.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u439	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Commercial Companies Code Act, contained in print No 3541. The purpose of the Government's submission is to increase transparency and efficiency of the exchange of information in tax matters in line with OECD standards, as well as to ensure that tax authorities can obtain and exchange information on shareholders of public limited liability companies and limited liability companies with bearer shares. It is also important to increase the security of trading in securities by introducing mandatory dematerialisation of shares. It should be stressed that the current construction of bearer shares guaranteeing the anonymity of the shareholder causes Poland to be included in high-risk countries, which do not guarantee the exchange of tax information about bearer shares holders, as it is not possible to identify them, which could lead to fraud in the trading of shares, for example in the context of money laundering. This problem is already partially resolved in the case of public companies, in which we are dealing with the actual transformation of bearer shares into registered shares. It is therefore possible to determine the owner after an analysis of the stock account maintained by the brokerage house, which is the result of the compulsory dematerialisation of the shares and the replacement of the document with an account record. The proposed regulations will lead to the establishment of a single legal framework by introducing an autonomous trading regime for shares of non-public companies. The project, maintaining the division between registered shares and bearer shares, introduces the legal basis for compulsory dematerialisation of shares. As a result, each action, whether it is a bearer or a name action, has de facto the status of a registered share allowing also the identification of a shareholder entitled to bearer shares in legal cases. The solutions contained in the government proposal go in the right direction. Therefore, on behalf of my club, I request that the proposal be directed to further legislative work in the committee responsible. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u440	Thank you very much, sir. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition - Mr Zdzisław Gawlik will speak. I invite you, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u441	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club, I wanted to present my position on the proposed law amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and some other laws. Basically, as the previous speakers have already mentioned, the proposed law is moving towards the introduction into the Commercial Companies Code of the basis for compulsory dematerialisation of shares of non-listed public limited liability companies and shares of limited liability companies. The introduction of the proposed amendment to the Commercial Companies Code will eliminate from our system of law, existing since 1934 in the Polish system of private law, the system of division of shares of the public limited liability company into registered shares and bearer shares. It must have been 85 years for someone to come up with this kind of solution. Dematerialisation is the actual liquidation of bearer shares and is a kind of circumvention of the law. My previous speaker, presenting the club's position with regard to the proposed legal act, said that we didn't eliminate those bearer shares. We're not eliminating, but we're putting in place a system where bearer shares remain only by name. The designer, having set up a goal, uses tools that may give rise to the false belief that nothing has changed with regard to the type of bearer shares in question. The analysis of the justification of this proposal leads to the belief that it is completely different. At the same time, it points out that there is a continuation of various changes or practices undertaken by the rulers over the last 3.5 years in various areas of social and economic life, which are the implementation of the idea that the state wants to know everything about everything and everyone. The introduction of this change undermines the state's confidence in a citizen who is deprived of anonymity in business. In the explanatory memorandum, the developer argues that the existing legal system makes it impossible to identify a shareholder and raises concerns about abuses, inter alia. with regard to the danger of money laundering. The wisdom of the Polish legislator of 1990, who introduced changes to the Civil Code concerning securities that created their general principles, was that the solutions adopted at that time not only defined nothing but also did not restrict the ways in which securities were created. This certainly gave great freedom and was used to create a security as an instrument to support economic development. As regards the regulation of the Civil Code, it must be added that in addition to registered securities and on request, bearer securities may also be created and created. The proposed change with regard to dematerialisation of registered shares tries to eliminate one type of share, one type of bearer security, but does not eliminate other types of bearer securities, i.e. essentially this assumed change of these effects does not bring. The proposed change, on the other hand, causes the differences between the limited liability company and the public limited liability company, as the Minister said. It does not appear that the circumstances in favour of dematerialisation are the elimination of the anonymity of the shareholder vis-à-vis the shareholder in the company of O.O. Nor does it seem advisable to refer to history or old doctrinal concepts to justify dematerialisation. If by dematerialization we want to align the status of partner of the company with the o.o. and a shareholder, this raises the question of why to maintain different differences, especially when they occur in the area of the creation or operation of companies of one or another type. Nor do they support the argument that dematerialisation would increase the safety and efficiency of trading in shares by reducing the costs of this trading and would even lead to its simplification and a better guarantee of relations in the company, as stressed by the designers. Consequently, the proposed change undermines the state's confidence in the citizen and eliminates its anonymity. And this, as I mentioned, occurred in the Polish system not only in relation to the public limited liability company and bearer shares, but also in relation to the so-called. a silent partnership, which has a permanent place in other legal systems, e.g. ct [[♪ The bell ♪]] the French system as Société anonyme. None of the advantages identified by the designers justifies support for this proposal. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition, I propose that the proposed amendment to the Commercial Companies Code be rejected at first reading. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u467	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and some other laws, print No 3542. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, addressed on 18 June 2019 the above draft law to the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development for first reading. At this point, I'm going to say a few words about the procedated bill. The main purpose of the Act is to adapt Polish legislation to the obligations arising from Regulation No 2018/302 of the European Parliament and of the Council on unjustified geo-blocking and other forms of discrimination against customers on grounds of nationality, residence or place of business in the internal market. This regulation is one of the documents of the European Commission's e-commerce package, published on 25 May 2016 in the framework of the initiatives announced in the Digital Single Market Strategy and the Single Market Strategy for goods and services. The draft law also provides for changes in the internal structure of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. It also introduces provisions allowing the President of the Office to have access to tax confidentiality in proceedings under the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and the Act against unfair exploitation of contractual advantages in the marketing of agricultural and food products and to banking secrecy in the case of the second of these laws. The Committee on Economic Affairs and Development, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meeting on 2 July 2019, requests the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u468	Thank you very much, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles.
