ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u469	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Gentlemen of Ministers! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club with regard to the government draft law amending the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and certain other laws, print No 3542. The draft law amending the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and certain other laws aims, first, to bring national law into line with the provisions of Regulation No 2018/302 of the European Parliament and of the Council on unjustified geo-blocking and other forms of discrimination against customers on grounds of nationality, residence or place of business in the internal market. The purpose of the geoblocking Regulation is to ensure that consumers, regardless of their nationality or place of residence or establishment in the European Union, better access to goods and services in the single market by preventing direct and indirect discrimination in cross-border e-commerce, when purchasing online as well as when travelling to other Member States for the purpose of acquiring goods or using services. The aim of this regulation is also to enable entrepreneurs to expand their business to new markets and to be interested in their offer of new customers. Secondly, it is about enabling the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection to be more flexible in relation to the state of the current internal structure of the office, in particular with regard to delegations. Currently, the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection is composed of a headquarters in Warsaw, laboratories supervised by the President of the Office of Competition and Consumer Protection and nine delegations with legally defined offices. The draft amendments aim to introduce greater flexibility in the creation and assignment of tasks by the President of the Office to off-road units, i.e. Delegations. The project involves regulating the tasks of the UOKiK delegations in the statutes issued by a regulation of the President of the Council of Ministers. Thirdly, the aim is to improve the effectiveness of detecting infringements of the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection by broadening the access of the President of the Office to legally protected secrets, such as fiscal secrecy in proceedings under the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and the Act against unfair exploitation of contractual advantages in trade in agricultural and food products, as well as banking secrecy in the case of the latter. Fourthly, it is about adapting the provisions of the Act on the Protection of Competition and Consumers and the Act on the Prevention of Unfair Use of Contractual Advantages in Trade in Agricultural and Food Products to the current legal situation. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I propose an amendment to the contested law giving a new wording to point 7 in Article. 1 and I recommend to the High Sejm that the Act on the amendment of the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and some other laws be adopted, print No 3542, together with the proposed amendment. I'll allow myself to put the amendment on your marshal's hands. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u470	Thank you very much, sir. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform - The Civic Coalition will present the position to Mrs Magdalena Ewa Marek. Come on in.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u471	Dear Marshal! High Seym! I would like to present the position of the Civic Coalition Platform club vis-à-vis the government draft law amending the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and some other laws, print No 3542. The main reason for the amendment is the alignment of national provisions with Regulation 2018/302 of the European Parliament and of the Council on unjustified geo-blocking and other forms of discrimination against customers on grounds of nationality, residence and place of business in the internal European market, which entered into force on 3 December 2018. These are important provisions from the point of view of a very dynamic, evolving and changing digital market. Dry data can show that over the last 4 years the number of Internet users who have sought cross-border access to content that is on the Internet has almost doubled. In 2015, it was an interest rate of 8%, 4 years later, i.e. in 2019, it was 15% . Given that these indicators are the highest among young people - up to 28% is in the age group of 15 and 24 years - it shows that the dynamics will grow from year to year. The prohibition of unjustified discrimination in online sales on grounds of nationality, place of residence or place of business is an embodiment of European values of equality between citizens of all Member States. This law is also important in order to encourage Internet users to reach out to content from a legal source. It is therefore necessary to adapt the Polish provisions to the provisions of the Regulation. It is precisely about imposing an obligation on the Member State to comply with the provisions of the EU Regulation, but also to equip it with instruments and the possibility of helping businesses and consumers in the event of a dispute. The President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection will be equipped with such instruments as amended by the Act, but also with common courts. The elimination of geo-blocking is another important and beneficial step for consumers and businesses to regulate the digital market following the Payment Services Directive, cross-border rules on services, parcel delivery and strengthening consumer protection for online purchases. The amendment also involves changes in the structure of the OKAiK itself: first of all, the delegation in Warsaw is liquidated, and duties will be taken over by the headquarters and by delegations in the field. The change is a similar application to similar institutions, such as the Electronic Communications Office or the Civil Aviation Authority. The Act will remove the provisions that refer to the seat or the operating principle of the OOKiK so that these issues are regulated by the statute or by the regulations of the work. Moreover, according to the amendment, each voivodship will have a representative in the National Council of Consumer Ombudsmans. There are now eight of them, this number will be increased to sixteen, so that each voivodeship will have its representative, it will not increase budget costs. The law provides for three months to be adapted. It should also be mentioned that the President of the UOKiK will be given additional authority - he will have access to the tax secrets of the Change of the agenda. In proceedings under the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and the Act against unfair exploitation of contractual advantages in the marketing of agricultural products, the Marshal will gain access to banking secrecy. So in the course of the proceedings, which will be conducted by the President of the UOKiK, he will have access to these two secrets. It hasn't happened so far. The changes mentioned in the UOKiK structure relate to the four-month period of vacathio legis, the period of implementation, and all other changes will enter into force 14 days after the announcement. In view of the above, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - the Civic Coalition supports the government's proposal to amend the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection and some other laws. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u473	High Seym! Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the club PSL-UED, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Competition and Consumer Protection. The project envisages the designation of authorities responsible for the enforcement of Regulation (EU) 2018/302 of the European Parliament and of the Council on unjustified geo-blocking and other forms of discrimination against customers on grounds of nationality, residence or place of business in the internal market. The provisions of the EU Regulation are intended to prevent discrimination against consumers wishing to buy products or services in another European Union country - either directly or via the Internet. It's about geo-blocking, so-called. geoblocking. Transport, financial and audiovisual services will be excluded from the provisions of the Regulation. The purpose of the Regulation is to ensure better access to goods and services in the single market, regardless of their nationality or place of residence or business in the European Union. The means of achieving this objective shall be to prevent direct and indirect discrimination in cross-border e-commerce (on-line purchases) and when travelling to other Member States for the purpose of acquiring goods or using services. The draft law serves to apply the provisions of the Regulation by designating competent authorities to enforce its provisions. The PSL-UED Club will present its position in the voting block.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u474	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Large international companies often introduce artificial segmentation of the market in the European Union along its internal borders. Such discriminatory practices significantly contribute to a relatively low level of cross-border transactions in the Union, including in the e-commerce sector, preventing the full potential of the internal market from being exploited. The EU regulation introduced by that law should limit discrimination on grounds of nationality, place of residence or place of business of customers, including unjustified geo-blocking, in cross-border transactions between traders and customers concerning the sale of goods and services in the Union. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u480	Mrs. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! As the rapporteur of the committee has already presented, at the June meeting, the Committee on Public Finance discussed the government draft law amending the Act on auditors, audit firms and public oversight and some other laws, print No 3481. There is no doubt that the purpose of amending the Act is to strengthen public oversight of the activities of auditors and audit firms in Poland, because supervision in the current formula did not function properly in all aspects. Events related to the activities of GetBack and the supervisory actions undertaken against that entity of public interest have highlighted the shortcomings of the current public oversight system over the quality of certification services provided by statutory auditors and audit firms, in particular in relation to capital market transactions. Currently, supervision of the quality of services provided by statutory auditors and audit firms in units of public interest is exercised by the Audit Supervisory Board, which does not have sufficient tools to control the quality of services provided by audit firms related to capital market transactions, as a significant proportion of the supervisory tasks have been delegated to the Polish Chamber of Auditors, which carries out quality checks on statutory audits of entities other than units of public interest, as well as carrying out investigations and sanctions in respect of them through disciplinary action and the National Disciplinary Court. The draft law presented by the government in place of the Audit Supervisory Committee is set up by a separate body - the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency, which will be a state-owned legal entity forming part of the public finance sector, designated as the competent public authority supervising the audit. It will be equipped with new supervisory, investigative and sanctioning tools for statutory auditor services subject to professional standards performed both in public interest entities and non-public interest entities. Therefore, the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency will carry out public supervision over the entire audit in Poland. The Polish Chamber of Auditors will carry out tasks within the scope of supervision to a limited extent. It will continue to have competence to establish national standards for the pursuit of the profession, national standards for quality control and the ethics of statutory auditors, as well as to control the compliance of statutory auditors with the obligations of compulsory vocational training and carry out the tasks relating to the approval and registration of auditors. It will also conduct disciplinary proceedings for offences other than those incurred in the performance of certification services and related services carried out in accordance with national standards for the pursuit of the profession by statutory auditors. The powers of the Financial Supervision Commission will remain unchanged. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I recommend the adoption of a draft law submitted by the government amending the Act on auditors, audit firms and public oversight and certain other laws, print No 3481. At the same time, on behalf of the club, I propose four amendments harmonising and clarifying certain provisions of the Act. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the Government's draft Print Act No 3481. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The draft is contained in print No 3495 and the report of the joint committees is published in print No 3575. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! There have been a number of problems in the field of waste management, especially in recent years, which have plagued all stakeholders, i.e. owners of real estate, who have to fulfil their duties, municipalities that carry out waste collection and disposal, companies in the industry. There weren't any of them. there was no clear incentive for selective collection of municipal waste, there were no significant differences in the rates of fees for segregated and non-segregated waste. We were faced with a situation such as the lack of incentives that would stimulate landowners to have home composters, due to the lack of possibility of reducing the fees for residents who process their own biodegradable waste fractions. Nor was there such clarity in view of the divergent case law of the administrative courts concerning the applicability of more than one criterion which would differentiate the rates of charges in municipalities and inter-communal associations. We have certainly had too little control of local government units over the municipal waste management system, including the supervision of entities that receive waste. It can be said without a doubt that we have been dealing with a monopolistic market for the collection of municipal waste. I have heard from my wójtów such information that after organizing the tender the price in a small, several thousand-dollar municipality increased by a million compared to last year's price and they are afraid to make another tender because one monopolist threatens to raise the price by another million. This was undoubtedly a very cumbersome situation that required change. It is also a very bad practice for waste managers to dump waste in places not intended for this purpose. It was equally important to seal the recycling levy system for all plastic bags. In 2018, I remind you, the recycling charge was imposed on light shopping bags, up to 50 Sm. This reduced the release of these bags, but at the same time some sellers replaced these thin plastic bags with thicker ones, avoiding the need for recycling charges. As a result, we had a situation that actually the amount of bags used was not significantly reduced. Also an important problem that this law solves is the lack of sanctions for not registering an imported car and not registering an end-of-life vehicle. This change is the result of formal allegations by the European Commission concerning the failure to transpose Directive 2000/53/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 18 September 2000 on end-of-life vehicles. The European Commission considered that the Republic of Poland did not ensure the effective application of the Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Article 2 and Article 2 Article 6 (6) (a) 1 Directive 2000/53/EC. In connection with the work at the Ministry of Infrastructure on solving the problem of the implementation of this directive, which was prolonged, this was included in the draft law discussed here, in Article. 4, so that at least the most urgent issues, which are the subject of the reasoned opinion of the European Commission, find a solution. High Izbo! There isn't much time to discuss such a comprehensive and multifaceted bill. In short, I will tell you what solutions have been put in place. That's, among other things.: making the possibility of using these criteria which differentiate the rates of charges in municipalities and inter-communal unions more flexible; introducing the possibility of exempting from part of the municipal waste management fee property owners who have home composters and compost biodegradable waste; clarifying in the provisions of the Act the time for reporting corrections and setting the deadline for the final report by the recipient of municipal waste to the municipality, by the municipality to the marshal's office, and by the Marshal's office to the Minister of the Environment, because this is how the waste management system works. There is also the addition of a provision for a 3-year period of non-entry into the register of traders who have been deleted from the register of regulated activities. Sanctions shall also be imposed for the submission of false declarations of compliance with the conditions for carrying out activities in this field by operators receiving municipal waste from owners at the time of application for entry in the register. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u519	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Minister, On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the joint committee on the government draft law amending the Law on Cleanliness and Order in municipalities and certain other laws contained in print No 3575. The original printing is printing number 3495. The bill the government is proposing is aimed at, among others. providing incentives for selective collection of municipal waste and strengthening the control of municipal waste streams by municipalities. Many changes are being made to the project, which will improve the system. It is proposed, among others. the statutory obligation for municipalities to ensure the selective collection of municipal waste, comprising at least paper, metal, plastics, glass, packaging waste, multimaterial and biowaste. For the sake of clarity, an amendment has been introduced concerning information on municipal waste management systems which the municipality has posted on the website or in a customary manner. A provision is proposed to allow the construction, expansion, modernisation, maintenance and operation by the municipality or in conjunction with other municipalities of waste treatment facilities resulting from the treatment of municipal waste in accordance with the waste hierarchy and proximity principle if such installations are not present on the local market or if existing installations have insufficient capacity. The revision of the legislation in this respect will allow the municipality to control the entire waste stream. The draft law extends the basic list of waste accepted by the municipal waste collection points by among others. household waste from medical supplies, i.e. needles, syringes, textiles and clothing and hazardous waste. In accordance with the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 28 November 2013, the draft law details the issue relating to the maximum rate of charges for the collection of waste from immovable property on which residents do not reside. This process introduces the possibility of exempting property owners from part of the fee, who, as the rapporteur said, are using biowaste, which is municipal waste in composters. This proposal will provide an economic incentive for the use of home composters by property owners. The proposed higher non-segregation charge is mainly intended to encourage residents to properly collect municipal waste. The Act introduces the provision that the provisions on the coverage of other plastic bags with a recycling charge will enter into force on 1 September 2019, which will reduce the practice of replacing lightweight plastic bags with thicker bags, also made of plastic. A very important and expected change is the introduction of an obligation to provide information on the addresses of sites receiving waste from farmers or waste treatment plants. This is very important in the case of municipalities in the area where the agricultural holdings are located, due to the huge demand to dispose of typical waste arising on these farms, i.e. Waste foil, cord and tires. The proposed amendments will have a positive impact on the environment and on human health and life. The proposed changes will improve the system of municipal waste management and thus improve the cleanliness and order of municipalities, which is why Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports, in accordance with the committee's 3575 report, the government draft law amending the Cleanliness and Order Law in municipalities and some other laws, the original print No 3495. The purpose of this law is primarily to break down the regional monopolies of municipal waste treatment facilities and freeze admission limits to these RIPOKs by voivodship waste management plans. Unfortunately, we have such phenomena that at the moment regional installations feel monopolistic, having a stream of waste provided, and if they want to have increased limits, the Marshal does not allow them to do so, so prices go up in an unlimited way - in no way can we justify more than twice the price increase, for example. from December 2017 to January 2019 No, wages have not doubled, although they have actually increased a lot. No other costs have increased that much. It is a monopolistic procedure, sometimes deliberate, sometimes forced by the Marshal's decision, which does not allow to increase the limits. [[There's no...]] This is the answer to the question of why prices are rising. That's the reason. That is why this law provides, firstly, for the abolition of regionalisation, secondly, for the possibility of building new installations, and thirdly for financial support for these installations in order for local authorities to take over waste management. Where local authorities - there are such examples - or local authorities are owners of the collection system and owners of regional municipal waste treatment facilities, prices are moderate. Indeed, local governments can do so with concern for the inhabitants, although sometimes local governments also behave monopolisticly. One of the reasons that the Constitutional Tribunal has pointed out is the change, or actually the introduction of an upper limit on non-inhabited property charges, i.e. holiday homes. The very different practice made it so great that it was disproportionate to the residents of the municipality. Therefore, among others. We are executing the judgment of the Constitutional Court. Determination of the fee of 10% The disposable income, i.e. about PLN 160, is, of course, due to the average amount of fees that were applied. Another thing is investment. Mr Stefan Romecki was worried about investment. Well, now that there's nothing to do with the waste, the municipalities or the companies taking waste from the municipalities have nowhere to transfer it to the RIPOKs, it's actually the investments that are at the moment, there's nothing to worry about. We allow these investments to arise. The next stage, which will create new investments, is to unfreeze those who have received limits in the voivodship waste management plans. Unfortunately, there's a lot of frozen limits. I understand that sometimes you can freeze the limit so that there's no competition, and that you hold the limit. This law provides that these limits, which are unused, which are paper and block the development of other companies, will disappear. Such an investment will be thrown out in the waste management plan so that others who really want to operate in the waste management system can enter this place. Of course, we also introduce national limits. It is clear that recycling is the first task in terms of waste management. We strive to achieve 65% recycling after many years. In this law, we set a limit for the production or extension of a thermal treatment plant of up to 30%. The point is not to build an incinerator that burns 100%. waste, because by no means will we obtain such a level of recycling. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u616	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the pleasure of presenting three draft laws. The first is the Government's draft Tax Order, print No 3517, which is part of the package of laws, whose assumption is to balance the interests of the fiscal and the taxpayer. The principles of the bill submitted and the original draft of the Act, prepared by the General Tax Law Codification Commission, have been the subject of work carried out in the Ministry of Finance since October 2017. The work on the new Tax Order focused on improving the existing rules, in particular on eliminating those malfunctioning, and adding new solutions to this act. Each provision has been analysed, taking into account how it will affect not only the rights of the taxpayer but also the exercise of the powers of the tax authorities. The tax authority must have real instruments for levying and collecting taxes without violating the rights of the taxpayer. The proposed act is intended to replace the current Tax Order. Tax law is developing very dynamically. The various phenomena and processes that are the subject of its regulation are constantly evolving. Tax legislation must keep pace with changing social and economic environments. This law has been in force for more than 20 years and has been amended more than 150 times at that time. Numerous amendments to the existing rules and judgments of the administrative courts and the Constitutional Tribunal show that the readability of the tax constitution, which is the Tax Order, has been affected. The proposal presented to the High House is fully in line with the new challenges. The new ordination respects the interests of the taxpayer and the treasury through institutions to protect the rights of the taxpayer in its relations with the tax authorities and institutions to enhance the efficiency and efficiency of the dimension and collection of taxes. The protection of the rights of the taxpayer is served, among others. general principles of tax law, e.g. the principle of information and support, the principle of presumption of integrity of the obliged person, the principle of legitimate expectations. An important instrument to protect the rights of the taxpayer will be the consensual forms of tax settlement, e.g. tax agreement, mediation, cooperation programme, consultation of the tax consequences of transactions. The project also contains a number of other solutions to protect the taxpayer in dealings with the tax administration. It is worth mentioning here: the limitation period for a tax liability; the prohibition of ruling against a taxpayer at the stage of the primary proceedings; measures to combat the length of proceedings; the right to correct the declaration before the tax procedure is terminated; the possibility of not appealing the decision to the court; longer time limits for appeal and complaint. Improving the efficiency of the implementation of tax obligations will enable, inter alia,: institution of limitation of the size and collection of tax obligations; simplified procedure; elimination of proceedings concerning amounts of tax up to PLN 50; principle of cooperation between the taxpayer and the tax authority to the extent resulting from tax legislation; dissemination of electronic communications in contacts with the tax authority. The project introduces a number of new institutions that do not exist in the new tax ordination. The most important of these are the cooperation programme, addressed to entities of significant economic importance; the tax agreement between the tax authority and the taxable person concluded in case of difficult doubts as to the facts of the case; mediation; the procedure for consulting the tax effects of transactions; a catalogue of the general principles of tax law applicable to the whole of tax law; the correction of declarations in the course of tax proceedings; the restoration of a material and legal period; the remission of the tax before the payment date; the prohibition of ruling against the taxpayer by the first instance authority; the general information on substantive changes to the tax legislation. The project also includes dozens of completely rebuilt institutions included in the existing tax ordination. This is a proposal for a completely new act, taking into account proven in practice, well-functioning regulation of general tax law. The project also includes the provisions currently contained in the Act on the Rules of Registration and Identification of taxpayers and payers. The project will result in strengthening the taxpayer's position in relations with the body, increasing the trust of taxpayers in the tax authority and faster, more efficient handling of tax matters. In presenting the above, I propose that the project be directed to further work. Mrs. Marshal! Since, as I understand, we are working together on three draft laws, I am still pleased to present a brief information on the draft law on the Ombudsman of Taxpayers' Rights. This is print 3516. This proposal provides for the establishment of an institution for the protection of the rights of the taxpayer to increase citizens' sense of security in their dealings with tax authorities. The new institution should contribute to the development of more transparent standards for taxpayers in the functioning of the tax administration. The draft law aims to strengthen the position of the taxpayer and therefore requires the creation of a specialised, decentralised institution that will comprehensively assist anyone who applies to the Ombudsman. In practice, it is in particular about strengthening the position of taxpayers who, in the event of a dispute with the tax authority, do not have the means to avail themselves of professional consultancy services, legal firms. It will be the task of the Ombudsman's institution to assist all taxpayers in the event of a threat to their rights, with the proposal assuming that the Ombudsman's direct assistance will mainly be addressed to taxable persons not engaged in business activities. In the case of traders, the Ombudsman will be obliged to cooperate in the protection of their rights with the spokesperson of small and medium-sized enterprises. This will further increase the pressure on tax authorities to respect business rights. The establishment of an institution for the Ombudsman's rights is of particular importance in the context of changes in the tax system arising inter alia. with a dynamically changing legal and economic environment as well as the need to implement EU directives. In particular, the Ombudsman, who is a high-level tax specialist, will uphold the principles of trust in public authority and the principle of impartiality, equal treatment, fair treatment, the principles of respect for the legitimate interests of taxpayers and the principles of the rationality of the action of tax authorities. The bill includes, among others, a simple catalogue of the rights of the taxable person which can be regarded as a specific card of the rights of the taxable person. This is a simple language catalogue of basic rights resulting from tax regulation. Such a solution is a normative expression of the ombudsman's idea that the right should be formulated in an accessible, understandable way and its application should be used to shape relationships based on trust between citizens and public authorities. The compilation of a simple, normative catalogue of taxpayers' rights with the establishment of an institution for the protection of taxpayers' rights gives comprehensive, real support to citizens in fulfilling their tax obligations. The draft law grants the Ombudsman numerous strong powers to carry out his statutory tasks effectively. In order to protect the rights of the taxable person, the Ombudsman will, for example, be able, first, to request the initiation of a tax procedure, to bring an action before the administrative court, including a cassation complaint to the Supreme Administrative Court or to participate in proceedings before an administrative authority or court. Secondly, it will be able to object to the final decision of the tax authority or to require the repeal or amendment of the final decision under the rules laid down in the provisions of the Tax Order. Thirdly, request the competent authorities to suspend enforcement or enforcement proceedings against the taxpayer. Fourthly, a request is made on the basis of the provisions of the Tax Order for a general review of tax legislation or tax clarification in cases of serious interpretation doubts. Fifthly, it will be able to apply to the competent authorities for a legislative initiative or to issue or amend other legislative acts in tax matters. The Ombudsman will keep the President of the Council of Ministers informed by the end of April each year of his activity and of the state of compliance with the rights of the taxpayer. This information will be published in the Public Information Bulletin on the website of the Ministry of Finance. It will also be the duty of the Ombudsman to draw up an annual report summing up the results of the analysis of general and individual tax laws and the analysis of the way in which tax authorities deal with complaints and requests. This report will be published each time in the Public Information Bulletin. The Ombudsman will carry out his tasks with the help of the Ombudsman's office. In addition to the office, there will be deputy spokespersons, representatives of the Ombudsman's area, in order to provide taxpayers throughout the country with the widest possible and universal access to assistance from the Ombudsman's institution. The special distribution of field workers will be decided by the Ombudsman, taking into account needs. The Ombudsman will be appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers for a 6-year term. The candidate for the Ombudsman will be selected in an open, competitive recruitment. The candidate for the Ombudsman shall present to the President of the Council of Ministers a minister competent for public finances of no more than two candidates fulfilling strict competence criteria. The same person will only be able to be a spokesman for one term. The draft law contains a closed and narrow catalogue of the reasons for the appeal of the Ombudsman, which guarantees his independence. In addition, in order to ensure impartiality, objectivity of the function performed, the person in question will not be able to carry out other work, other occupations, including business activities, except for teaching, research, research and teaching tasks in higher education. The Ombudsman will not be a member of a political party, a trade union or a public activity that cannot be reconciled with the dignity of the office occupied. The proposal provides for the appointment of the Ombudsman for the first term of office no later than 1 April 2020. By submitting the above to the High House, I propose that the submitted proposal be submitted for further work. The third draft is the draft law Law introducing the Tax Order and the Act on the Ombudsman's Rights, print No 3518. It aims to maintain the coherence of the bill Tax Order and the bill on the Ombudsman's Rights. The replacement of the current Tax Order by the new law requires the introduction of adjustments to tax laws, non-tax laws, which are subject to the arrangements provided for in the Tax Order. Adaptation changes are also necessary in connection with the Taxpayer's Rights Act. The project shall equip the Ombudsman with the legal tools necessary to carry out the statutory tasks and ensure harmonious cooperation with other bodies. The project provides for the introduction of a mechanism for providing local tax support to local tax authorities in the application of local tax legislation and tax legislation. This support will be provided by the National Tax Information, which is planned to create a tax competence centre for local taxes. The Centre's task will be to assist local tax authorities in the handling of individual cases by providing explanations and carrying out procedural replacements without entering their competence. Support will be granted only at the request of the local tax authority. In addition, the project provides for strengthening the procedural position of the local tax authorities by giving them the power to challenge decisions and provisions of the local review colleges before the administrative courts. The project also grants these authorities the right to challenge the interpretation of the individual finance minister amending or repealing the individual interpretation of the local tax authority. The project will ensure the smooth application of the current new Tax Order law during the transitional period. The project will also have a positive impact on the handling of individual cases by local tax authorities. The draft sets the date of entry into force of the Act on the Ombudsman's Rights on 1 January 2020 and the Tax Order on 1 January 2021. In presenting the above, I propose that the submitted project be submitted for further work. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u618	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I present the opinion of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice concerning a package of government billings including the draft Tax Order Act, print No 3517, draft law Provisions introducing the Tax Order Act and the Act on the Taxpayer's Rights Ombudsman, print No 3518, and draft law on the Taxpayer's Rights Ombudsman, print No 3516. The current Tax Order, despite numerous amendments, does not follow the direction of the transformation of economic processes, leading to fiscal fraud due to numerous negative procedures in the tax system. To e.g. to reduce tax avoidance, in particular in international trade, or to be able to effectively seal the tax system, there is a need to change the rules in the Tax Order, which will keep up with the changes in economic processes and eliminate the negative effects on the state budget. The provisions of the new Tax Order introduce into the Polish legal order regulations resulting also from the guidelines of the European Union for the exchange of tax information, the sealing of the tax system, transparency in the granting of tax preferences and public aid and the like. The European Commission, recommending in 2012 that solutions be put in place to counter aggressive tax planning practices leading to tax avoidance, already pointed to the obligation for Poland to adopt, as a member country, legal solutions adapted, among others. to the national situation ensuring the fight against tax fraud. The Government of the Civic Platform and the PSL had a duty to inform the Commission within 3 years. Of course, the government has not taken any action in this regard. Changes in ordination also force the fast-growing digitalisation of tax procedures and the growing electronic communication that improves contacts with the taxpayer. Taxpayers themselves are also waiting for changes in the Tax Order, mainly due to the numerous, often controversial judicial case law of the Constitutional Court and the Court of Justice of the European Union and due to the rapidly growing number of individual tax rulings. The authors of the draft Tax Order propose the introduction into the new legal act of the existing Tax Order, which functions well, are effective and tested, which have been developed and improved over the years by case law and practice. The solutions proposed by the Government in the draft Tax Order to the High House have two main objectives: firstly, to protect the rights of the taxpayer in his relations with the tax authorities, and secondly, to increase the efficiency and effectiveness of tax collection. The draft of the ordination contains legal solutions to mitigate excessive rigour of tax ordination towards the taxpayer. In a situation where more and more new legal instruments are emerging in trade not always to improve turnover, the government proposes to introduce a number of mechanisms and procedures to ensure that tax authorities can be more effective in enforcing statutory tax obligations in order to ensure that their tax revenues are received into the budget. In view of the increasingly complex and complex economic and social phenomena, a new Tax Order is needed in order to introduce effective tax regulation. Globalisation of the market and increasing cross-border trade foster the development of increasing tax optimisation, including cross-border ones. The tax optimisation process significantly reduces tax revenue. In order to protect budgetary revenue and meet the requirements imposed on Member States by the European Union, the Government proposes in the draft Tax Order to maintain the existing provisions setting out the general clause against tax avoidance. It proposes that, while maintaining the scope of its application so far, following minor changes in certain detailed arrangements, the new legislation should be adopted in the current wording. In Polish tax law, the clause has only been in force since 16 July 2016, although the European Commission already drew attention to the risks associated with tax evasion in its recommendations of 6 December 2012, and then recommended that the Polish Government introduce a tax avoidance clause. Also after the audit carried out on 17 April 2015 by the Supreme Audit Chamber, which issued the opinion that the lack of a clause in Polish law is the reason for the lack of tightness of the Polish tax system. At that time, in the audit protocol, NIK presented the amount of corporation tax revenue in respect of gross domestic product in the years 2008 to 2013 and showed that the receipts in those years were decreasing from year to year. In 2008, it was 2.72% , and in 2013 - only 1.79%. In the description of this phenomenon, NIK noted that the decrease could have been caused by the dissemination of tax avoidance measures. In the same document, it showed that CIT revenues were also falling. At that time, the House requested the Government of the Civic Platform and PSL to implement solutions to counter the declining income tax base. For the first time, the clause in Polish law was introduced on 1 January 2003, but the Constitutional Tribunal ruled by judgment of 11 May 2004 that it did not meet the constitutional standards of decent legislation and repealed it. The general anti-tax avoidance clause proposed in this proposal complies with the requirements laid down in Directive 2016/1164 of the European Council of 12 July 2016. The provisions of that Directive shall be implemented by Member States by 1 January 2019. The general anti-tax avoidance clause introduced in the Tax Order ensures equal treatment of taxpayers on the market and the implementation of the constitutional principle of equal taxation at both the level of formation and the application of the law. This draft codification commission was forwarded to the Finance Minister, as I said, in October 2017, but the Council of Ministers adopted the new Tax Order only on 28 May 2019, with numerous modifications to the solutions proposed by the Codification Board. ParlaMint-PL_2019-07-03-sejm-83-1.u634	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present a statement on the uprising of the 25th Infantry Regiment of the Home Army on Piotrków soil. During World War II, Piotrkowska's land witnessed many events that disappeared in collective memory, and today it is functioning only on pages of few historical studies. Inevitably, heroes leave, who might be like them and witness to them. One such event is undoubtedly the creation in mid-July 1944 of 25. Army Infantry Regiment. The name was not a coincidence: in Piotrków before the war, the 25th infantry regiment was stationed, which was famous for the battles with the Bolsheviks in 1920, and later in the September campaign in 1939 he fought in the composition 7. The Infantry Division near Częstochowa. The unit was formed from the subordinate commander of the Kedywo district of Łódzkie AK Capt. Adam Trybus has troops of the Piotrkowski inspector. This was part of the implementation of the shares of the Storm. The concentration of the regiment took place in Barkowice Wetrych, and at that time the unit counted less than 600 soldiers. During its several months of existence, the branch has undergone several reorganisations, adapting to the changing situation in its area of activity. On August 16, 1944, the unit fought a large guerrilla battle with two groups of German soldiers and gendarmes in the vicinity of the hills bearing the designation on military maps: 285, and by the local population called the Devil Mountain. Into an ambush organized by Lt. Kazimierz Załęski ps. A few dozen Germans came by. A criminal expedition of nearly 400 soldiers was sent to help this group. These forces came to 360 guerrillas from the 25th National Army regiment under Captain's command. Hilary Moraczewski ps. Morus. They were forced to retreat and lost dozens of soldiers. The unit was active until November 1944, in the meantime carrying out with various happiness a series of bravado actions, such as the attack on the Slucze in September 1944, the fight against the Niemojewski Mountains and many others. In the apogee of its existence, the regiment had about 1,250 soldiers. In November 1944, the regiment was deformed for reasons of loss. The balance of the individual's activities is difficult to grasp. It is estimated that on the side of the Germans as a result of the regiment's actions there were over 1000 killed and about 400 wounded soldiers. Their own losses are only about 120 soldiers. About the 25th fight. The National Army Infantry regiment today resembles the obelisk in Barkowice Wetrych and a monument on the road from Sielpi Wielki to Horses. Thank you.
